# Preregistered analysis plan (confirmatory runs)

_Author: independent methodology/statistics reviewer (stats-reviewer subagent), 2026-10-11. Saved verbatim by the coordinator because subagents cannot write .md report files._

Status: DRAFT for coordinator approval. Becomes binding when its SHA-256 and the final-task manifest hash are written to experiments/registry.jsonl BEFORE any untouched-final-set rollout is scored. Anything not specified here is exploratory and must be labelled so. Evidence: analysis/out/sim_*.md|json (scripts analysis/scripts/sim_*.py, run on the real core/cap_evolve code; pure stdlib, run with python3.12 -I). Items marked [PLACEHOLDER] must be re-estimated from the development pilot before freeze (edit constants in sim_mde.py).

## 1. Estimands
Arm a = an artifact (seed artifact, one-shot repair, or champion of method m under search seed s). y_a(t) = expected reward of a on task t over execution randomness; observed task score = mean of valid trials. All contrasts are paired on tasks.

| id | name | definition | supports | interval must include |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | fixed-task-set score | theta1(a) = (1/T) sum_t y_a(t) over THE untouched final set | "on this split a scores X, beats b by D" | execution randomness only |
| E2 | generalization to new tasks/families | theta2(a) = E_t[y_a(t)] over the population the final set was drawn from (new families for transfer suite) | "optimized artifacts transfer" | task sampling (family-clustered) + execution |
| E3 | optimization-pipeline variance | distribution of champion E2 score over search seeds for fixed method, budget, start | "method reliably improves", best-of-S vs typical | search-seed sampling (S searches) + tasks within each |
| E4 | cost | section 9 | amortized value | search-cost variance across seeds, deployment-cost sampling |

Primary headline per suite: E2 advantage of each method's champion over the seed artifact, reported with the E3 spread. E1 goes in benchmark tables but is never offered as evidence of generalization. ONE shared baseline measurement of the seed artifact serves every contrast on a suite (avoids the 76.5/76.8 and 38.5/38.6 discrepancies).

## 2. Data hierarchy
- Unit of inference: the task, clustered by family. Trials are only averaged within a task; every task has weight 1 regardless of trial count.
- Families: tasks sharing an instruction template, source workbook or generator; ids from the data steward's manifest, fixed before scoring.
- Search seed: one independent end-to-end optimizer run from the same starting artifact, split and budget. Final-set evaluation seeds are independent of search seeds.
- Crossed/paired design: every (arm, seed) champion scored on the same final tasks.
- S=3 seeds gives 2 df. Pipeline-level intervals use t(S-1) (4.30 at S=3). Seed-level percentile bootstraps are NOT the headline when S<8.

## 3. Estimators
Paired difference d(s,t) = ybar_A(s,t) - ybar_B(t), baseline row broadcast over seeds.

| estimand | point | SE | interval |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 per arm | mean_t ybar(t) | sqrt(sum_t se_t^2)/T, se_t = trial SD/sqrt(n_t); needs n_t>=2 | normal +-1.96 SE |
| E1 contrast | mean_t d(t) | sqrt(sum_t(se_A,t^2+se_B,t^2))/T | normal |
| E2 contrast conditional on the S champions | mean over (s,t) of d | family-cluster-robust (CR1) SE of seed-averaged task deltas | t(C-1) if C<30 families else normal; cluster bootstrap as check |
| E2 pipeline contrast (claim about the optimizer) | mean over s of per-seed mean delta | variance of per-seed means (df=S-1) combined with task-cluster component | t(S-1) on per-seed means (headline); crossed "task+seed" bootstrap as companion |
| E3 spread | sample SD of per-seed champion E2 scores | n/a | chi-square df=S-1; at S=3 sigma in [0.52 s, 6.3 s]; always report the S raw values |

Implemented in analysis/scripts/paired_cluster_bootstrap.py (paired_cluster_bootstrap, signflip_pvalue, holm); families resampled jointly across arms and seeds. Self-test (12 families, 60 tasks, 3 seeds): percentile bootstrap covers 0.86, size 0.14; CR1 t covers 0.93, size 0.075; ignoring clustering covers 0.69. Rule: below 30 families the headline interval is cr_ci, not the percentile bootstrap.

### 3a. Why not stats.combined_stderr
stats.py:35-53 returns sqrt(var(task means)/T + sum se^2/T^2). Variance of observed task means already contains within-task noise, so the second term adds it again. sim_stderr.md (mu .55, kappa 1.5, T=40/280):

| n trials | combined^2/Var(new tasks) | combined^2/Var(fixed tasks) | coverage combined vs new-task target | coverage between-only vs new-task target |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 1.00 | 1.6 | .94-.95 | .94-.95 |
| 3 | 1.33 | 3.9 | .97 | .94-.95 |
| 5 | 1.24 | 5.4 | .96-.97 | .94 |
| 10 | 1.13 | 8.6 | .96 | .94-.95 |

New-task estimand: variance overstated 12-33% at kappa 1.5 (up to 63% at kappa 5, n=3), SE 6-28%; between-task term alone is correct. Fixed-task estimand: variance overstated 1.2-8.6x; only the within term is right. At n=1 the within term is 0 so combined_stderr silently equals between-only. Paired gate SE (gate.py:430-437) matches new-task contrast variance (ratio 1.00); unpaired `significant` mode (gate.py:461-462) uses 1.5-6.2x more variance. Rule: the paper never reports SplitResult.stderr. E2 SEs come from cluster-robust task-level deltas; E1 SEs use the within-only formula (n_t>=2) or the E1 interval is not reported.

## 4. Reporting vocabulary
- SD: spread of individual units, always labelled with its unit ("SD across tasks", "SD across search seeds, n=S"). Never used to express uncertainty about a mean.
- SE: SD of the ESTIMATOR over repeats of the sampling process named in the estimand (execution for E1; tasks/families for E2; seeds for E3). Always states which.
- CI: 95% interval from the section 3 estimator. Template: "mean paired difference +0.062, 95% CI [0.021, 0.103] (cluster-robust, 56 families, 280 tasks, 3 seeds averaged)". Not a statement about where the true value lies; not a prediction interval.
- Range/prediction interval: only for E3, raw per-seed values plus mean +- t*SD*sqrt(1+1/S), labelled with S.
- Dev-val numbers (gate deltas, val curves, posterior probabilities) are search diagnostics, never results; under the one-stage gate reported gains are ~4x the true gain on new tasks.
- Every table cell states n tasks, n families, n seeds, n trials/task; no table mixes estimands in a column.

## 5. Inference and multiplicity
- Comparison family frozen with this plan, one row per (suite, contrast): e.g. each of 4 search methods vs seed artifact, hill-climb vs agent-optimize, each method vs matched strongest-competitor arm, per primary suite. Unlisted contrasts are exploratory, no p-values in the abstract.
- Holm step-down at FWER .05 within a suite; each suite is its own family; a cross-suite claim needs both to pass.
- Primary test per contrast: two-sided cluster-robust t (cr_p_two_sided) at E2 level; sign-flip randomization p (signflip_pvalue) as pre-declared robustness check. If they disagree, report both; do not resolve post hoc.
- "Win" language only if Holm-adjusted p<.05 AND CI excludes 0 under the pessimistic missingness bound of section 6. Otherwise "no detectable difference at the MDE of X".
- Non-inferiority (cost/latency): margin fixed before freeze; claim needs one-sided 97.5% lower bound above -margin.
- Ablations: Holm within each ablation family or labelled exploratory.
- No result may be added, dropped or re-run based on outcome. A crashed/aborted search stays in the denominator; its champion is the seed artifact unless a candidate had already been accepted.

### 5-AMENDMENT 1 (2026-10-11 21:15 UTC; written before the search cutoff 2026-10-13 06:00 UTC; no final-set data seen)

Supersedes the sentence "each method vs matched strongest-competitor arm" above (it is data-dependent). Everything else in section 5 stands. Reason: review item A2 (TRIAGE.md); the first version had no pre-specified CapEvolve-vs-baseline test.

**5A. Primary (confirmatory) family, per suite.** All contrasts paired by task, cluster-robust (CR1 t, section 3) at the matched checkpoint B*, Holm within the suite. CapEvolve arms are hc (hill-climb, rule P) and ao (agent-optimize, rule P). Tier 1 baselines are the matched-budget Tier 1 arms actually run on the suite; "cheap" arms are not in the primary family.
- Suite A (12 rows): {hc, ao} x {seed, GEPA, SkillOpt, Meta-Harness, WikiSkill, EvoSkill}. No one-shot control on A.
- Suite B (8 rows): {hc, ao} x {seed, one-shot, GEPA, HarnessLens}.
- Suite C (6 rows): {hc, ao} x {seed, one-shot, GEPA}. Suite C is development-exposed: reported with p-values but never the sole basis of a generalization claim.
A baseline row that has an incomplete seed under the B* rule (B* rule in decisions.md) leaves the family and the Holm denominator shrinks accordingly; the reason is reported. No arm is added after the cutoff.

**5B. Secondary families (own Holm each, reported as secondary):** (i) every Tier 1 and cheap baseline vs seed, per suite (A 8 rows, B 2, C 1); (ii) hc vs ao, and each CapEvolve arm vs each cheap arm (Suite A: ACE, MIPROv2, Trace2Skill). Superiority is the only hypothesis; no equivalence claim is made without a pre-stated margin.

**5C. Exploratory (no p-values in abstract or headline):** E12 (stock gate vs rule P, Suite C only), Tier 2 arms, footprint and merge ablations, any contrast not listed above.

**5D. Secondary analysis: native end-of-run artifacts.** For each arm, the artifact the method would deploy at the cutoff under its own native rule, with budget NOT matched (arms with budget-coupled schedules, e.g. SkillOpt cosine learning-rate schedule, GEPA max-metric-calls, iteration-capped arms, are named in the paper). Same rows as 5A, own Holm family per suite, labelled "unmatched budget". The primary analysis stays at matched B*. If the two analyses disagree in sign or in significance for a 5A row, both are reported side by side and the primary stands.

**5E. Unit of the rule-P false-promotion rate.** The probabilities in section 8 and in the paper (0.040 for B, 0.113 for C, 0.068 for A at option c) are P(at least one false promotion in one search), where a search screens about 7 to 10 candidates; the per-candidate rate is roughly 0.6 to 1.3% (1-(1-P)^(1/N)). They are simulation results under stated assumptions, not guarantees.

**5F. Observed variance replaces the assumed MDE inputs (section 7 is superseded for B and C).** `analysis/out/mde_observed.md` re-estimates trial noise and between-task variance from the unmodified seed artifact on the search splits: Suite A val-40 (6 evaluations; within-task variance 0.088; observed evaluation-to-evaluation SD of the split mean 0.088 vs 0.047 binomial, i.e. an evaluation-batch SD of about 0.075), Suite B opt-49 (15 trials/task; within var 0.148, SD of true p 0.31, kappa 1.5) and Suite C opt-20 (9 trials/task; within var 0.124, kappa 0.9). Measured paired null SD (0.55 B, 0.50 C) matches sqrt(2 x within var). The between-task SD tau of the true advantage cannot be identified from baselines; MDEs are tabulated for tau in {0, 0.10, 0.15, 0.25}. Consequence: with 3 seeds and final sets of 70 / 40 / 20 tasks the conditional (artifact-level) MDE at tau = 0.15 is 0.12 (A, n=1), 0.16 / 0.125 / 0.11 (B, n = 1 / 2 / 3) and 0.22 / 0.17 / 0.15 (C); pipeline-level MDEs are 0.24, 0.31 and 0.41 (n=1), and under the Holm first-step level they are larger still. Suites B and C are therefore descriptive transfer-of-protocol suites; Suite A is only informative for large effects. All arms of a suite must be scored interleaved in random order in one window, because the Suite A batch effect would otherwise raise the conditional MDE at T=70 from 0.12 to about 0.27.

**5G. Rule-P operating point at the actual task counts.** `analysis/out/sim_budget_{A,B,C}.md` (real `promotion.screen/decide`, 550 counted search rollouts on B and C, 600 on A, up to 10 candidates, observed difficulty). Option c (n_conf 3, z_conf 1.282, dmin 0) has P(>=1 false promotion per search) 0.040 (B, screen on opt-49), 0.113 (C, opt-20), 0.068 (A, val-40) and power at a true +0.08 edit of 0.33, 0.17 and 0.40. Option n_conf 5 / z 1.645 is not used because one confirmation costs 2 x T x n_conf rollouts: 400 of 600 on A, 490 on B (more than the 452 left after the baseline and one screen, so no promotion is ever possible: power 0), 200 on C; on A and C it lowers the expected true gain of the champion relative to option c (A +0.0007 vs +0.0014; C +0.0015 vs +0.0024) together with the false-promotion rate.

### 5-AMENDMENT 2 (2026-10-11 21:15 UTC; before the search cutoff; no final-set data seen)

Adds an evaluation-window rule and a time-block sensitivity analysis. Reason: the six evaluations of the SAME seed artifact on Suite A val-40 gave split means 0.55, 0.575, 0.525 (hill-climb) and 0.45, 0.425, 0.65 (agent-optimize), an SD of 0.076 against 0.047 expected from trial noise (permutation test of run-to-run excess variance, p = 0.02). The hill-climb vs agent-optimize difference (0.550 vs 0.508) is NOT significant (permutation p = 0.42) and no harness difference was found (decisions.md, 2026-10-11 21:15 entry), so the excess is evaluation-window variance, not an arm effect.

**5H. Final scoring window.** Within each suite, every rollout of the final evaluation (all primary, secondary and native-artifact arms, the seed artifact, and the one-shot control) is scheduled in one interleaved, randomized order (seeded; the order file is hashed before the first rollout) inside a single window. The seed artifact is re-scored inside this window with the same number of trials per task as the arms (at least 2). The window is as short as capacity allows; if it must be suspended, the interruption is logged and counts as a block boundary. Every rollout records start and end timestamps, the deployment queue time and the arm, which the existing per-rollout metrics already provide.

**5I. Time-block sensitivity (pre-specified).** Rollouts are assigned to blocks of about 1 hour by start time (blocks with fewer than 5% of the rollouts are merged into the neighbouring block). Two analyses are computed for every row of 5A and 5B:
- primary: the unadjusted paired CR1 contrast of section 3;
- block-adjusted: the same contrast with a block fixed effect, i.e. each arm's per-task trial scores are centred by the block mean of the seed artifact's task-demeaned scores in that block, then averaged as in section 3.
Drift check on the seed artifact inside the window: regress the seed's task-demeaned trial scores on block (or on start time as a linear term) with task-clustered SEs and compute the largest between-block contrast and the linear change over the window. If either exceeds 2 SE, the block-adjusted estimate becomes the headline for that suite and the unadjusted one is reported beside it; otherwise the unadjusted estimate stays the headline and the block-adjusted one is reported as a sensitivity. The drift check uses only the seed artifact's scores, never the contrasts, so it cannot depend on the outcome of any comparison. The check and the rule apply to Suites A, B and C separately.

**5J. Consequences stated for the paper.** Arms are never scored in separate sessions; scores from search-time evaluation (different times, different load) are never compared across arms.

**5K. Secondary descriptive outcome: empty-output rate (added 2026-10-11 21:20 UTC, before the cutoff).** For every arm in the final evaluation, and for the seed artifact scored inside the same window, report the fraction of rollouts that end with no output file (target-side raw tool-call text such as `<|tool_call>call:python_interpreter` emitted as the last assistant turn, so nothing is parsed or written), per suite where applicable. Definition is mechanical (empty `Rollout.output` and no error flag) and applied blind to the reward. It is descriptive only: no test, not in any Holm family, and such rollouts stay scored as failures under section 6 (they are neither infrastructure errors nor retried). A fallback parser or other harness fix found by a method is a legitimate artifact edit, not a parity breach; the rate table shows whether an arm found one. The runner also probes the raw-tool-text rate hourly on a fixed 5-task canary (seed artifact) so a provider-side change during the window is visible; the probe results are reported in the appendix and used only to interpret the block check of 5I.

**5L. Planned final-set sizes (pinned in decisions.md, 2026-10-11 22:30 UTC).** Suite A T = 70 tasks, n = 1 trial per task for each arm and n = 2 for the seed artifact; Suite B T = 40, n = 3; Suite C T = 20, n = 3. The MDEs for these sizes are the `planned` entry of `analysis/out/mde_observed.json` (3 search seeds, alpha .05, power .8; vs-seed contrasts use noise = within var x (1/n_arm + 1/n_seed)). At the central tau = 0.15 the conditional MDE (the 3 frozen champions) is 0.106 on A, 0.110 on B and 0.152 on C, and the pipeline-level MDE (t with 2 df) is 0.221, 0.225 and 0.292; at the Holm first-step level the conditional values are 0.142, 0.143 and 0.196. Noise-only floors (tau = 0): 0.087, 0.086, 0.116. Contrasts between two non-seed arms on Suite A (both n = 1) use noise = 2 x within var and have a conditional MDE of 0.117 at tau = 0.15. tau and the seed-level SD are not identifiable from baselines (section 5F), so these are planning values, not power guarantees.

### 5-AMENDMENT 3 (2026-10-11 22:45 UTC; before the search cutoff; no final-set data seen)

Prompted by reviewer R4 on draft 2: a non-significant result must not be read as "no difference", intervals must say which claim they support, the stock-gate ablation needs a pre-set comparison, and readers must see whether a search changed the artifact at all.

**5M-1. Equivalence rule (the only licence for "no meaningful difference").** For each row of the primary family 5A and secondary families 5B whose superiority test is not significant, run a TOST on the artifact-level contrast (the conditional CR1 estimate of section 3: mean paired difference of the 3 frozen champions vs the comparator, cluster-robust, t with T-1 df). Margin m = 0.10 reward points on Suites A and B (absolute; about the conditional MDE of the planned sizes, 0.106 on A and 0.110 on B at tau = 0.15, so equivalence is claimable only when the data could in principle support it; a practitioner-relevant difference is 10 points). Suite C is descriptive only: no equivalence claim (conditional MDE 0.15, so a 0.10 margin would be unreachable). Decision: "equivalent within +-0.10" only if both one-sided tests reject at alpha = .05 (equivalently the 90% CI lies inside (-0.10, +0.10)); "superior/inferior" only by the 5A/5B Holm test; otherwise "inconclusive". The words "no difference", "matches", "as good as" are not used without a passing TOST. TOST p-values (the larger of the two one-sided p) get their own Holm family per suite, over the rows to which TOST was applied. Pipeline-level equivalence is never claimed at S = 3: the 90% pipeline CI half-width (t with 2 df, about 0.2) exceeds the margin.

**5M-2. Which interval supports which claim.** Every primary row reports two intervals side by side:
- artifact-level (headline for tests and Holm): CR1 on task-level deltas of the S frozen champions. Supports only "these S artifacts, as found in these runs, differ from the comparator by D on the task population".
- pipeline-level: t(S-1) interval with SE^2 = SE_task^2 + s_seed^2 / S, where SE_task is the CR1 SE of the seed-averaged task deltas and s_seed^2 is the variance across seeds of the per-seed mean deltas (conservative: the seed-by-task part is counted in both terms). Supports "the method, rerun from other seeds, would produce an advantage of about D".
Any sentence that generalizes beyond the three artifacts (about the method, the optimizer, "X beats Y") must be backed by the pipeline-level interval; if that interval includes 0 the sentence is restricted to the frozen artifacts. Abstract and conclusions state the artifact-level scope explicitly. Per-seed values are shown for every arm.

**5M-3. E12A and E12C (stock-gate ablation, exploratory).** E12A: CapEvolve hill-climb with the stock gate (paired k_se = 1, footprint masking on, candidate-side coverage 0.6, no rule P) on Suite A, 3 seeds, launched with the same splits, budget, isolation and logging as E10 hc; E12C is the same on Suite C. Comparison point per suite: min(B*, the arm's own counted rollouts at the cutoff) for the stock-gate arm; the hc (rule P) artifact it is compared with is taken at that same rollout count. Contrasts: hcstock vs seed and hcstock vs hc (rule P), each as artifact-level CR1 mean difference with 95% CI and per-seed values, descriptive only: no Holm, no p-values in the abstract, not in any family of 5A/5B. If hcstock stops short of B*, the shorter count is stated beside every number.

**5M-4. Promotion count per search (reported for every arm).** For every arm and seed at the comparison point: the number of candidates screened, the number promoted/accepted under the arm's native rule, and whether the returned artifact is byte-identical to the seed artifact (content hash). Table in the main text next to the scores (an arm that returned the seed on most seeds is visible); per-seed values in the appendix. Descriptive only; no test. Arms without a native accept notion report artifacts whose content hash differs from the seed.

### 5-AMENDMENT 4 (2026-10-11 23:05 UTC; before the search cutoff; no final-set data seen)

**5N. B* is computed over primary-family arms only** (decisions.md, 23:00 UTC entry): the matched checkpoint B* of each suite is the largest rollout count reached by every seed of every arm that appears in the primary family 5A. Secondary and exploratory arms (cheap arms, E12A/E12C, Tier 2) are compared at min(B*, their own counted rollouts at the cutoff); where that is below B*, the shorter count is disclosed per seed beside every number. The B* rule of decisions.md (rounded down to a multiple of 50, floor 200) is otherwise unchanged.

### 5-AMENDMENT 5 (2026-10-12 02:11 UTC; before the search cutoff; no final-set data seen)

Amends items 2, 4 and 5 of section 6 for the final evaluation. Evidence: a Meta-Harness candidate (recalc_verify) produced "sandbox exec failed: 500" on 17% of its rollouts against 5% for other candidates, so an error the uniform classifier labels infrastructure can be induced by the artifact, and treating it as missing would favour artifacts that break the sandbox.

**5O-1.** Every infrastructure-classified error is retried up to 3 times with new trial seeds (unchanged). A cell that is still an infrastructure error after the third retry (`infra_final`) counts as a FAILURE (reward 0) in the PRIMARY analysis, because persistence across fresh seeds implicates the artifact (or a task the artifact cannot run), and the same rule applies to every arm and to the seed artifact. In the sensitivity analysis `infra_final` cells are treated as missing and handled as in section 6 items 4 and 5 (complete case plus pessimistic/optimistic bounds). If the primary and sensitivity conclusions differ for a 5A row, both are reported and the primary stands.

**5O-2.** Hangs past 3 h keep the existing rule: one retry, then missing (also in the primary analysis). Neither rule changes the classification of artifact-induced failures (section 6 item 3, scored 0).

**5O-3. Reporting.** Per arm and per suite: the number of cells touched by each rule (infra error at first attempt, recovered by retry 1/2/3, `infra_final` scored 0, hang retried, hang missing), with the total cells.

**5O-4. Diagnostic (descriptive, no test).** For every scored artifact, the infrastructure-error rate (first-attempt rate and `infra_final` rate, with Wilson 95% intervals) next to the rate of the seed artifact scored in the same window (amendment 2, 5H). An artifact whose rate clearly exceeds the seed's is flagged in the appendix with the dominant error text; the flag changes no estimate and no test.

### 5-AMENDMENT 6 (2026-10-12 08:40 UTC; written before any final-set data, DISCLOSED AS MADE AFTER SEEING SEARCH-TIME SELECTION-SPLIT RESULTS)

What was seen: `analysis/out/searchtime/summary.md` (selection-split numbers of each method's own search, not held out). They show that CapEvolve with rule P is nearly inert (most seeds on A, B and C return the seed artifact), while CapEvolve hill-climb with its stock gate (E12A on Suite A, E12C on Suite C) promotes and shows selection-split gains comparable to competitors. Following the decision that the paper is a CapEvolve method paper whose shipped configuration is the stock gate (decisions.md, 2026-10-12 08:33), this amendment adds a family built on information that depends on that look. It is therefore labelled post-search-time throughout the paper. Nothing in the primary family 5A changes, and none of the specification below uses any final-set outcome. N* is the matched checkpoint called B* above.

**5P-1. Secondary confirmatory family S-stock, per suite, own Holm.** CapEvolve hill-climb with the stock gate (hcstock) at the comparison point min(N*, its own counted rollouts at the cutoff), count disclosed per seed:
- Suite A (6 rows): hcstock vs seed, vs GEPA, vs SkillOpt, vs Meta-Harness, vs WikiSkill, vs EvoSkill.
- Suite C (2 rows): hcstock vs seed, vs GEPA. Suite C stays development-exposed and is never the sole basis of a generalization claim.
- Suite B has no stock-gate arm and no row.
Same paired, cluster-robust artifact-level test and the two intervals of 5M-2; the comparator artifact in each row is taken at its own matched count. The family is secondary: it is reported in the main table with its own Holm-adjusted p, labelled "post-search-time". hcstock vs hc (rule P) stays descriptive (5M-3) and is not in the family. The equivalence rule 5M-1 applies to non-significant rows.

**5P-2. Selection-to-final shrinkage (winner's curse), every arm.** For each arm and seed with a searched artifact: shrinkage = selection-split gain minus final-set gain, where the selection-split gain is the champion's score minus the seed artifact's score on the split on which the arm made its own selection decisions (stock CapEvolve val; GEPA its selection set; the arm's own selection set otherwise), both taken from the search record at the comparison point, and the final-set gain is the champion minus the in-window seed artifact on the final set (paired, section 3). Reported per arm: the per-seed values, their mean, and a 95% interval with SE^2 = SE_sel^2 + SE_fin^2 (task-clustered SEs of the two independent task sets; t with min(T_sel, T_fin) - 1 df, conservative), plus the seed-level SD. Interpretation fixed in advance: a positive shrinkage is the selection-induced optimism (including the regression to the mean of the one-time search-time seed measurement); a gain "survives" only when the final-set artifact-level interval excludes 0 (5A/5P-1), not because the shrinkage is small. Seeds that return the seed artifact contribute shrinkage 0 by construction and are marked. Each arm is tested once (H0: shrinkage = 0, two-sided t) in an own Holm family per suite; no test compares shrinkage between arms; between-arm differences in shrinkage are descriptive.

### 5-AMENDMENT 7: SUITE D PRE-REGISTRATION (2026-10-12 09:20 UTC; before the first Suite D rollout)

Machine-readable copy: `experiments/configs/suite_D_registration.json`; hashes in `experiments/registry.jsonl` (written by `experiments/tooling/register_suite_d.py`). Suite D is a self-contained ablation family and is never pooled with Suites A to C. **Disclosure:** it was designed after the search-time results of Suites A to C and the `E00-azprobe` seed-baseline probes (search-split task ids only) were seen. No Suite D rollout, held-out score or final-set outcome exists at this point. Sections 3, 5M, 5O and amendments 2 and 5 apply unchanged unless stated here.

**7A. Setting.** tau2 retail (E10B builder and splits: opt-49, sel-25, sealed test-40). Target `azoai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`; user simulator and judge `azoai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`; temperature 0, max tokens 8000. Optimizer Sonnet 5.5, hill-climb. Search on opt-49 (train = val); 550 counted rollouts, at most 10 iterations, 4M non-cache-read optimizer-token guard; 3 seeds per cell. Reference gate = the stock gate (paired k_se = 1, footprint masking on, candidate-side coverage 0.6, errored-trial policy fail). Each cell ends natively (budget, iterations or guard); the comparison point is the cell's own end of search, disclosed per seed (no B* truncation inside Suite D; the one-shot control has 200 rollouts by construction).

**7B. Tier 1 cells (confirmatory).** E20-P (prompt surface only), E20-T (tools only), E20-PT (prompt + tools; the REFERENCE), E20-CTL (one-shot: a single proposal, no selection), E21-ruleP (PT surface, rule P with the E10 parameters), E21-1S (PT surface, one-stage gate with footprint masking off and joint coverage, no confirmation), E20-GEPA (prompt only, 550 counted rollouts, selects on sel-25). The surface lock `TAU_SURFACES` makes a locked surface pristine; candidates that differ from the seed on a locked surface are counted as `surface_violations` per cell and reported. Exploratory cells if run (descriptive, no p-values, no Holm): E20-TD (descriptions-only emulation), E23-nohist, E23-nodigest, E21-ruleP-2x, E20-PTS, E24-AO, E27-airline.

**7C. Final set and scoring.** H65 = sel-25 + test-40 (T = 65) for every CapEvolve cell (no CapEvolve gate reads either). All champions and the seed artifact are scored in ONE interleaved randomized window (amendment 2): n = 5 trials per task for Tier 1 champions, n = 10 for the seed artifact, sel-25 rescored inside the window. Scores of sel-25 taken at the end of a search are descriptive only (shrinkage 5P-2, E26) and no run, launch or design decision, including Tier 2 launches, may use any sel-25 or test-40 score of a Suite D cell. GEPA selects on sel-25, so every contrast involving GEPA uses test-40 only (T = 40) on both sides. Missingness: section 6 and amendment 5 (retries, `infra_final` = failure, hang rule). A search that promotes nothing returns the seed artifact and stays in the denominator.

**7D. Confirmatory contrasts.** Unit and tests as in sections 3 and 5M-2 (task = cluster, paired, artifact-level CR1 with t on T-1 df is the test; the pipeline-level interval is shown beside it).
- Primary Holm family D, m = 10: (1) P vs PT, (2) T vs PT, (3) P vs T, (4) E21-ruleP vs PT (rule P vs the stock gate), (5) E21-1S vs PT (one-stage unmasked vs the stock gate), (6) PT vs CTL (search vs one proposal), (7) P vs GEPA-P (loop quality at matched surface, test-40), (8) P vs seed, (9) T vs seed, (10) PT vs seed.
- Secondary family D-sec, own Holm, m = 4: E20-GEPA vs seed (test-40), E21-ruleP vs seed, E21-1S vs seed, E20-CTL vs seed.
- Descriptive (CI and per-seed values, no p-values): interaction PT - P - T + seed (difference of differences, seed-level resampling), selection-to-final shrinkage per cell (5P-2; selection split = opt-49), promotions per search and returned-seed indicator (5M-4), surface violations, E25, E26.
- Equivalence: 5M-1 with margin 0.10 for every non-significant row of D and D-sec. A margin of 0.05 is not used (below the planning MDE, it could not pass).

**7E. Planning MDE (conditional on the 3 champions, tau = 0.15, 80% power; within-task variance 0.162 from the probe null SD 0.57, to be re-estimated from E22 without changing any rule or margin).** Contrast vs seed, T = 65, n = 5 (seed n = 10): 0.072 (Holm first step for m = 10: 0.096). Cell vs cell: 0.080 (0.105). Test-40 only: 0.093 vs seed, 0.102 cell vs cell. Pipeline-level (t, 2 df) vs seed: 0.165. Differences below about 0.10 between cells are therefore reported as inconclusive unless the TOST passes.

**7F. E22 null pool (descriptive, no test).** The unmodified seed is evaluated K = 40 times on opt-49 (1 trial per task per evaluation, tag `E22-nullpool`), before any candidate exists. Offline replay drives the real `gate.decide` and `promotion.screen/decide` on pairs of pool evaluations as null candidates (one evaluation = a screen; confirmation draws use further distinct evaluations; evaluations are distinct within a decision and reused across decisions). Gates: stock (empty footprint), one-stage unmasked, rule P (k 1, n_conf 3, z 1.282, dmin 0). Reported: false-accept probability per decision and per 10-candidate chained search, with a bootstrap interval over the K evaluations (resample evaluations as positions) and the ratio to the simulated rates of `sim_budget_B.md`. Also reported: per-task null SD, within-task variance and the evaluation-to-evaluation SD of the split mean (noise floor for every cell). The stock-gate row covers null edits with no footprint only and is labelled so.

**7G. E25 and E26 (descriptive).** E25: for each PT, E21-ruleP, E21-1S and CTL champion that differs from the seed on both surfaces, the P-only and T-only sub-artifacts (champion policy with seed tools, and the reverse) are scored in the final window (n = 3 on H65) and compared with the full champion and the seed: paired deltas with artifact-level 95% CIs and an additivity check delta(PT) vs delta(P) + delta(T); no p-values, no Holm. E26: candidates of E20-PT, E21-ruleP and E21-1S scored on sel-25 (n = 2) with the seed (n = 10) give descriptive false-accept and false-reject rates per gate with Wilson intervals; E26 scores never feed a decision.

**7H. Freeze.** The registration JSON, this plan, the experiment design file, the cell configurations and the order files are hashed into the registry before launch; deviations are logged in `decisions.md` and affected analyses are labelled exploratory.

### 5-AMENDMENT 8: SUITE D PRIMARY SET AND THE NO-HISTORY CELL (2026-10-12 09:42 UTC; before any Suite D held-out score and before any E26 rollout; no Suite D final data exists)

Changes amendment 7 as stated; everything not mentioned stays. It is a CHANGE from amendment 7C, made because reviewer R4 found that sel-25 is not sealed (its ids are visible in the search project and a search-end score is observed) and that GEPA selects on it. Verified facts: no CapEvolve gate reads sel-25 (the hill-climb gate screens and confirms on opt-49; `harness.finalize` scores the already-chosen best once), so the concern is process and wording for CapEvolve cells and a selection effect for GEPA.

**8A. Primary set.** Every contrast of Holm families D and D-sec (7D) is scored on the sealed test-40 only (T = 40), on both sides, for all cells.
**8B. H65 is secondary.** H65 = sel-25 + test-40 is still scored in the same final window (same n), and reported as a secondary, descriptive analysis labelled 'includes the 25 selection tasks of GEPA and the unsealed sel-25', with its own Holm over the same contrasts. It never overrides the primary.
**8C. Unchanged.** n = 5 trials per task for Tier 1 champions and n = 10 for the seed artifact in one interleaved window (amendment 2, 5H), the contrasts of 7D, TOST margin 0.10, the missingness rules, and the rule that no decision uses any sel-25 or test-40 score.
**8D. Planning MDE on test-40** (mde_observed.mde, within variance 0.162, tau 0.15, 3 champions, 80% power): vs seed 0.093 (Holm m = 11 first step about 0.127), cell vs cell 0.102 (about 0.139), pipeline-level vs seed 0.197; floors at tau = 0: 0.064 and 0.076. Any TOST or equivalence result that does not pass is worded 'inconclusive'.
**8E. Disclosure.** Written after the R4 review and before any Suite D held-out score. GEPA contrasts were already test-40 only.
**8F. New cell E26-nohist.** Identical to E20-PT except that the proposer receives no history: no LEDGER, JOURNAL, RUNMAP or prior_iterations, no insights accumulators, no protect/passing block (one flag `optimizer.ablation.history: false` / env `CAPEVOLVE_HISTORY=0`, default on, changing only what the proposer sees; gate, evaluator and scoring untouched). 3 seeds, same split, target, judge, stock gate and 550 rollouts. The code differs from the E20-PT runs only by that flag; the E20-PT seeds run on their own commit and both commits are recorded in the registry (items `suite_D_wt_tau_commit` and `suite_D_nohist_commit`). The contrast 'nohist vs PT' is added to primary Holm family D, which becomes m = 11, scored on test-40. Descriptive extras: pass-to-fail regressions vs the seed, accepted candidates per search, optimizer tokens per accepted candidate. Planning MDE about 0.10 cell vs cell; effects below it read 'inconclusive'. The cell is registered before its first rollout; its launch and any E26 data cannot change this amendment.

**5-AMENDMENT 8 ERRATUM (2026-10-12 09:49 UTC; stats-reviewer; no Suite D held-out score exists).** (i) Exact Holm first-step MDEs for 8D (test-40, T = 40, n = 5, seed n = 10, tau = 0.15, S = 3, 80% power, family D with m = 11, critical level 0.05/11): 0.1253 vs seed and 0.1377 cell vs cell (the "about 0.127" and "about 0.139" of 8D are replaced). Without correction: 0.0932 and 0.1024; with m = 10: 0.1241 and 0.1364; floors at tau = 0 with m = 11: 0.0855 and 0.1028. (ii) Text checked against intent: 8A to 8E agree with amendment 7 as changed by the R4 finding; the secondary family D-sec (m = 4) is unchanged and also scored on test-40. (iii) Naming: "E26-nohist" in 8F is the history-ablation cell; it is unrelated to "E26" in 7G (gate ROC on candidates scored on sel-25). Both labels stay as registered; in the paper the cell is called "no-history" and the gate ROC "gate ROC". (iv) Interpretation of 8F: the contrast "nohist vs PT" compares cells run on two code commits that differ by the history flag only; the registry records both commits.

### 5-AMENDMENT 9 (2026-10-12 09:55 UTC; written after paper review round 3 (draft 4, reviews/paper_draft4/TRIAGE.md section 10, coordinator decisions D3 to D6 and D9 of 2026-10-12 about 09:58 UTC) and after the search-time results were seen; no held-out or final-set score of any suite exists)

Changes only what is stated. Where it conflicts with 5P-2 (inert seeds contribute shrinkage 0), 5M-1 (equivalence scope) or the abstract-level wording of earlier amendments, this amendment governs.

**9A. No single headline estimand (D3).** The paper has no single headline estimand. The abstract reports a per-suite table of the CapEvolve configurations that were run (hill-climb with rule P; hill-climb with the stock gate; on Suite D also the one-stage unmasked gate), each vs the seed artifact, with the artifact-level and pipeline-level intervals of 5M-2 and its inert fraction (9D). The stock gate is named as the shipped configuration; its rows belong to the post-search-time family S-stock (5P-1, 9G) and Suite D family D, not to the primary family 5A, and are labelled so. Every row keeps the family and Holm correction it was registered under; the table adds no new test.

**9B. Cross-suite rule.** A claim that spans suites about one configuration or contrast is made only for the suites in which it was run, and requires the row to pass in EVERY suite named (Holm-adjusted p < .05 in that suite's own family, CI excluding 0, and the pessimistic-bound condition of section 6). Suite C is development-exposed and may be named but never carries a cross-suite claim alone. A claim that passes in one suite and not in another is reported per suite.

**9C. Amendments table.** The paper and the plan list every amendment with date, timing relative to data, and what had been seen (approximate wall-clock from the commit history and decisions.md):

| # | date (UTC) | content | data seen at the time |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 2026-10-11 (2e4989341) | base plan, sections 1 to 10 | nothing |
| 1 | 2026-10-11 21:15 | fixed contrast families 5A to 5D, observed-variance MDE 5F, rule-P operating point 5G | seed-artifact evaluations on search splits and rollout counters of running searches; no candidate outcomes or scores of searched artifacts |
| 2 | 2026-10-11 21:15, 21:20, 22:30 | interleaved window, block sensitivity (5H to 5J), empty-output rate 5K, planned sizes 5L | same, plus the six seed-artifact evaluations on Suite A |
| 3 | 2026-10-11 22:45 | equivalence, claim intervals, E12 contrasts, promotion counts (5M) | same, plus reviewer round on draft 2 |
| 4 | 2026-10-11 23:05 | B* over primary-family arms (5N) | rollout counters |
| 5 | 2026-10-12 02:11 | infra_final = failure (5O) | candidate-level sandbox error rates of one competitor's search artifacts |
| 6 | 2026-10-12 08:40 | stock-gate family and shrinkage (5P) | search-time selection-split results, rule P inert (post-search-time) |
| 7 | 2026-10-12 09:20 | Suite D pre-registration | search-time results of A to C, E00-azprobe seed baselines on search splits; no Suite D rollout |
| 8 (+erratum) | 2026-10-12 09:42, 09:49 | Suite D primary on test-40, no-history cell | review round on draft 3; no Suite D held-out score |
| 9 | 2026-10-12 09:55 UTC | 9A to 9G | review round 3 (draft 4); search-time results; no held-out score |
| 9G (E12B) | launched 2026-10-12 about 10:00 UTC, registered in this amendment | stock-gate hill-climb on Suite B (E12B) added to family S-stock; E12C best-of-k truncated | search-time results of A to C incl. E12A/E12C; no held-out score |

**9D. Seed-identical (inert) artifacts (D4).** An artifact whose content tree hash (tree_sha256 of the artifact manifest) equals the seed artifact's is INERT. (i) Primary analysis: its per-task deltas are set to exactly 0 (the method returned the seed); the re-measurement of such an artifact never enters a contrast. (ii) Measurement check, labelled as such: all inert artifacts of a suite have the seed's hash, so the seed artifact is scored once per suite as an extra labelled cell 'seed-check' with the arms' n, and its difference from the in-window seed (n = 10) is reported as an empirical null of window noise and a check that identical hashes behave identically. It feeds no test. (iii) Equivalence: a TOST row is not evaluated ('not testable, inert') if either arm is inert in at least 2 of its S seeds, because the interval then reflects the zero-by-construction deltas; a row in which both arms are fully inert reads 'both returned the seed'. For other rows the inert fraction of each arm is printed beside the verdict. (iv) Superiority tests include the structural zeros: returning the seed is a result of the method. (v) Shrinkage (5P-2) is computed over non-inert seeds only, with the inert fraction beside it (replaces 'inert seeds contribute shrinkage 0'). (vi) Cost per point = total optimizer cost over all seeds / total final gain over all seeds (gain per seed = artifact-level paired mean delta, inert = 0), in USD, optimizer tokens and rollouts per 0.01 reward points. Interval: bootstrap resampling seeds and tasks (families as clusters); if more than 2.5% of draws have total gain <= 0 the interval is reported as unbounded and the point value as 'no detectable gain', never a mean over positive seeds.

**9E. Equivalence scope and power (D5).** The margin stays +-0.10 (5M-1). Equivalence is claimed only at artifact level and only on Suites A and B (and on the registered Suite D rows, whose contrasts and margin are fixed in 7D and 8); never on Suite C and never at pipeline level. For every TOST row the paper reports the design power of the TOST at true difference 0 and at +-0.05 computed from the row's observed SE (stated as design power, not the power of the observed effect). A TOST that does not pass reads 'inconclusive'.

**9F. Suite B and C agent-optimize (D6).** B-AO and C-AO (agent-optimize on Suites B and C) could read the gold actions of their own opt and sel tasks. They stay in their primary families (5A) and carry a dagger in every table, figure and sentence where they appear; each such table has a sensitivity row or panel recomputing the family WITHOUT them (B: 4 rows, C: 3 rows, Holm rescaled) and reporting both. If primary and sensitivity disagree for a row both are shown and the primary stands (as in 5O-1). The isolation claim in the paper carries one sentence stating this.

**9G. E12B and truncated best-of-k (D9).** E12B (stock-gate hill-climb on Suite B, 3 seeds, identical to E10B hill-climb except the gate: paired k_se 1, footprint masking on, candidate-side coverage 0.6, no rule P) is added to family S-stock as a post-search-time secondary arm, launched 2026-10-12 about 10:00 UTC, before any held-out score. For Suite B the S-stock family (own Holm, m = 3) is E12B vs seed, vs GEPA and vs HarnessLens (the Suite B Tier 1 baselines), with the same paired cluster-robust artifact-level test, the two intervals of 5M-2 and the equivalence handling of 5M-1/9E. It does not bind N*. It is evaluated at N* if it reaches it, otherwise at its end-of-search artifact, labelled 'truncated at k rollouts' with k disclosed per seed. hcstock vs hc (rule P) on Suite B stays descriptive (5M-3). E12C best-of-k (exploratory, Suite C) was paused to free gpt-oss capacity for E12B. It is reported as exploratory, truncated at its pause counts (recorded by tau-runner), with descriptive CIs against the seed, no test, no Holm and no headline use; the pause count is printed beside every number.

**8G. Manipulation check for E26-nohist (registered before launch).** The proposer sandbox of the E20 cells (`runs/E10B/_sbx_bin/claude`) mounts the whole run directory read-write, so the proposer's cwd (`run_main/work/cand_N`) has the raw rollouts and candidate snapshots of earlier iterations, the earlier work dirs (with their LEDGER and JOURNAL), the earlier proposer transcripts (`claude_cfg/projects`) and `events.jsonl` one `ls ..` away; the path is also in its cwd, environment and the rejected.jsonl pointer. Switching the framework history files off therefore does not by itself remove the history from reach. After every E26 search, and for E20-PT as the comparison, `experiments/tooling/e26/scan_history_reads.py` counts proposer tool calls (Bash command, Read/Edit/Write/Grep/Glob arguments in the saved transcripts) that name LEDGER, JOURNAL, RUNMAP, prior_iterations, INSIGHTS, `run_main/(candidates|rollouts|native_sims|events|eval_index|state|baseline|history)`, `/frozen/`, `rollouts_metrics`, `claude_cfg`, `../..`, or another candidate's work dir, per seed and per iteration. The counts are reported per seed beside the E26 contrast (descriptive, no test, no exclusion). A seed with a non-zero count is reported as 'history reachable and consulted' and the nohist-vs-PT contrast is shown with and without it.
**8H. In force (coordinator approved 2026-10-12, before the first E26 rollout): hidden-history sandbox for E26 only.** The ablated factor is therefore "history unavailable: neither staged nor reachable"; the E20-PT proposer has history both staged and reachable. A real-claude pre-launch test (two iterations, 4 opt tasks, throwaway dir) passed: no LEDGER, JOURNAL, RUNMAP, INSIGHTS or prior_iterations text in the workdir files, INSTRUCTIONS, PROCESS, CLAUDE.md or staged guidance; `ls ..` shows only the own workdir; rejected.jsonl readable; both transcripts copied out; 0 history hits. The test also found three leaks that the first patch missed (PROCESS.md template line, end-of-run META_INSIGHTS sentence, diagnose-skill bullet); fixed in code commit 5357548685e7b11b6f4b636bd79305fcad1b8b20, which supersedes 35c30062b. `experiments/tooling/e26/claude_nohist_shim.sh` is the E10B shim with one change: instead of the whole run directory only the proposer cwd (rw), the project directory (ro), a per-invocation copy of rejected.jsonl and a per-invocation config directory are mounted, so candidates, rollouts, events, other work dirs, earlier transcripts, `frozen/` and the metrics files do not exist for the proposer; the new transcripts are copied out to `claude_cfg/projects` after each call so 8G can scan them. If approved, E26 differs from E20-PT by the history flag AND by this sandbox (both disclosed). **8H addendum (2026-10-12, before any E26 candidate was accepted or any second-iteration proposer call):** the claimed ablation had one further carry-over channel: a child of an ACCEPTED candidate starts from that candidate's snapshot, and `_SNAPSHOT_IGNORE` (harness.py:3698) does not list PROCESS.md or DIAGNOSIS.json, so the child workdir begins with the parent's filled PROCESS.md (clusters, edits, reasoning) and DIAGNOSIS.json (demonstrated on E20-PT s0: work/cand_0002/PROCESS.md = candidates/cand_0001/PROCESS.md plus the appended iteration). The E26 shim now resets PROCESS.md to the history-free template (`experiments/tooling/e26/PROCESS.seed.md`) and deletes DIAGNOSIS.json before every proposer call. DIAGNOSIS.json is written by the proposer itself (optional, harness.py:1455-1460; validated advisory-only at harness.py:3366); in the E20 cells (PT, P, T, ruleP) no proposer wrote one in the transcripts checked, in E26 two of three seeds wrote one at iteration 1, a behavioural difference that is reported with the 8G counts.

**9H. Suite D surface-lock violations (2026-10-12 10:00 UTC; added to amendment 9 before any held-out score).** A seed whose champion edits a surface outside its cell's lock (E20-P: anything but the policy; E20-T: anything but the tools; recorded per cell in `surface_violations.json`) stays in its cell and is analysed as intended (intention to treat: the cell is defined by what it was told to edit). It carries a dagger in every table and figure, and every contrast involving that cell (rows of families D and D-sec, 7D and 8F) gets a sensitivity row recomputed without the violating seeds (seeds removed, Holm denominator unchanged, the number of seeds per cell disclosed); if primary and sensitivity disagree both are shown and the primary stands. E25 decomposes each champion by the surfaces it actually edited (from the content diff against the seed), not by the cell label. The violation counts per cell are reported with the cell.

**5-AMENDMENT 9 ERRATUM 4: counted rollouts (2026-10-12 10:55 UTC; before the search cutoff 2026-10-13 06:00 UTC and before any held-out score).** Counted rollouts (for N*, budgets and the matched checkpoint) are rollouts consumed under the method's own search budget: seed or baseline evaluations, screens, confirmations and native selection evaluations. Post-search finalize and selection rescoring, chaser scoring and final-evaluation rollouts are excluded. Implemented in `experiments/final/bstar.py` (0169da3bd). Effect: the tau CapEvolve agent-optimize counts drop by their finalize rows (Suite B ao 198/197 to 148/147; Suite C ao 110/110/71 to 100/100/61); no other arm changes.

**10A. Setting and split.** tau2 airline @8ebb7499; target `azoai/gpt-4.1-mini-2025-04-14`, user simulator and NL judge `azoai/gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, temperature 0, max tokens 8000. Split file `wt-tau/protocol/tau_airline_splits.json`, sha256 `371ab03ba85cf25913f31d37b727077a5d6bcccce7f7c7ca983a3fc5d61e6187`: opt-20 and sel-10 are the official train-30, test-20 is the official test. Code: worktree `wt-airE` commit `eb112a08c75e24d60711377c2cfbf3265b86ecd8` for every cell.
**10B. Cells (3 seeds each, hill-climb, Sonnet 5.5) and budgets.** E30-PT (reference; prompt + tools, stock gate), E30-P (`TAU_SURFACES=policy`), E30-T (`TAU_SURFACES=tools`), E30-CTL (one iteration, 100 rollouts, no selection), E31-ruleP (rule P: k 1, n_conf 3, z 1.282, dmin 0, coverage 0.95, footprint off, joint coverage), E31-1S (one-stage unmasked), E30-GEPA (official GEPA 0.1.4, prompt only, selects on sel-10), E36-nohist (history flag off; E26 sandbox). Budget 300 counted rollouts and 10 iterations per search (counted as in amendment 9 erratum 4). Competitors for family E-comp are frozen by a later registry line, before the first competitor rollout; a competitor that cannot finish by the cutoff leaves the family with the reason recorded.
**10C. Primary set.** Official test-20 only (T = 20), n = 5 trials per champion and n = 10 for the seed artifact, one interleaved randomized window (amendment 2, 5H to 5I). The secondary set H30 = sel-10 + test-20 (T = 30) is rescored in the same window and reported as descriptive, labelled 'includes the selection tasks of GEPA', with its own Holm over the same rows; it never overrides the primary.
**10D. Families.** Family E, Holm m = 11, a mirror of family D (7D, 8F): P vs PT, T vs PT, P vs T, ruleP vs PT, 1S vs PT, PT vs CTL, P vs GEPA, P vs seed, T vs seed, PT vs seed, nohist vs PT. Secondary family E-sec (own Holm, m = 4): GEPA, ruleP, 1S, CTL vs seed. Family E-comp (own Holm; rows = E30-PT vs each competitor that runs). Same tests and intervals as D (artifact-level CR1 and pipeline-level), task = cluster. Equivalence: as for Suite C (9E), no equivalence claim is made on Suite E, because with T = 20 a TOST at margin 0.10 has design power of about one half even at zero true difference; TOST results are shown with their design power and read 'inconclusive' when they do not pass.
**10E. Claim rule.** Suite E only corroborates Suite D. A Suite D contrast is 'replicated' if the matching Suite E contrast has the same sign and its artifact-level 95% CI excludes 0; otherwise 'not replicated' (this includes inconclusive). No pooling of D and E, no cross-suite headline or p-value; E-only contrasts without a D counterpart (competitors) are labelled replication-domain and exploratory for generality.
**10F. Planning MDE (conditional on the 3 champions, tau = 0.15, S = 3, n = 5 champion / n = 10 seed, 80% power, t on T-1 df).** Within-task variance from the airline probe: null paired SD 0.22 per task, i.e. 0.0242 per trial (0.0484 per trial pair; the 0.0475 in the design draft is the pair variance). Test-20: 0.111 vs seed and 0.119 cell vs cell uncorrected; 0.153 and 0.164 at Holm m = 11; pipeline-level (t, 2 df) 0.222 vs seed. H30: 0.089 and 0.095 uncorrected; 0.121 and 0.129 at m = 11; pipeline-level 0.189. Noise-only floors (tau = 0): test-20 0.050 / 0.066 (m = 11: 0.069 / 0.091). The signal is clean (14 of 20 seed tasks are always 0) but with T = 20 task heterogeneity, not trial noise, sets the MDE; differences below about 0.15 are inconclusive.
**10G. Exposure.** Airline test-20 is development-exposed; Suite E results are labelled replication and carry no sealed-set claim.
**10H. Capacity rule (operational, changes no analysis).** E starts only when E22 has finished and the 15-minute azoai-mini 429 rate is under 2%; waves are admitted by that rule. If E is run on a different target to obtain quota, it is a different, non-replicating suite and needs its own amendment.

**5-AMENDMENT 9 ERRATUM 6: E12B paused (2026-10-12 11:42 UTC; before the search cutoff and before any held-out score).** E12B (stock-gate hill-climb on Suite B) was paused at 11:41 UTC on 2026-10-12 under the coordinator's load rule, because the primary Suite B hill-climb and GEPA rollout rates were still more than 10% below their 09:53 UTC baseline. Nothing persisted (the baselines had not returned), so the pause changes no data. It relaunches from scratch when all three E10B hill-climb seeds have ended their search, or when the gpt-oss queue median stays under 20 s for 30 minutes; the relaunch time will be appended in a further erratum. The evaluation rule of 9G is unchanged: min(N*, own counted rollouts), labelled 'truncated at k rollouts' if short, k disclosed per seed.

**5-AMENDMENT 10 ADDENDUM (2026-10-12 11:52 UTC; before the first Suite E rollout; no Suite D or E held-out score exists).** Requested by experiment-designer after the user chose to run Suite E on `azoai/gpt-4.1-mini` with all 8 cells x 3 seeds, admitted as Suite D frees capacity. Items 10I to 10L; 10H is replaced by 10J.
**10I. Search-end rule.** Suite E has no matched checkpoint and no cross-suite N*. Its searches may run past 2026-10-13 06:00 UTC up to their own budget (300 counted rollouts and 10 iterations; CTL 100 rollouts). Each champion is the cell's own end-of-search artifact (the last accepted candidate, else the seed). The final evaluation of Suite E follows when its searches end, inside the campaign window (before about 2026-10-14 05:00 UTC), in its own interleaved window (amendment 2). Because Suite E searches and launches may happen after Suite D is scored, no Suite E launch, wave, cut or design decision may use any held-out score of Suite D or Suite E (extends 7C).
**10J. Admission (operational, changes no analysis; replaces 10H).** The coordinator capped mini-gateway concurrency at 15 and the 429 rate is 0.0% since 11:00, so saturation shows as queueing. A wave is admitted when: E22 has finished AND the 15-minute azoai-mini 429 rate is under 2% AND the 15-minute mean mini `queue_s` is under 3 s AND Suite D's calls per minute per active D search are at least 85% of the baseline recorded at the last wave start; otherwise the next wave is held. At most 4 rollouts per search and at most 12 concurrent E searches (about 48 in flight). The check is `scripts/E00/admission_check.py` (hashed in the registry), run every 15 minutes by tau-runner.
**10K. Waves and cut order.** W1: E30-PT, E30-P, E30-T. W2: E31-ruleP, E36-nohist. W3: E30-CTL, E31-1S, E30-GEPA. W4: competitors. If time runs out, cuts follow this fixed order and are decided by the clock and capacity only, never by any score: competitors, E30-GEPA, E31-1S, E30-CTL, seeds 3 to 2 per cell, E36-nohist, E31-ruleP; E30-PT, E30-P and E30-T are never cut. A cut cell leaves its family (the Holm denominator shrinks), the reason and the time are logged, and a cell with 2 seeds is analysed with S = 2 (pipeline-level interval t with 1 df, reported as uninformative).
**10L. Family E-comp (competitor list frozen).** Rows: E30-PT vs each of the following competitors that completes its search within its own budget (300 counted rollouts), scored on test-20 with the E30 settings, own Holm over the rows present: ACE (3 seeds, tags `E32-ace-s<k>:ace`, policy addendum), MIPROv2 (3, `E32-mipro-s<k>:mipro`, policy addendum), GEPA full surface (3, `E32-gepafull-s<k>:gepafull`, policy + tools; its tools reflection-prompt template deviation is registered when final), CapEvolve agent-optimize (3, `E32-ao-s<k>:ao`, policy + tools; it can read the gold actions of its own train-30 tasks, so it carries the dagger and the sensitivity row of 9F). HarnessLens (1 seed, `E32-hl-s0:hl`, 300 counted rollouts, 4M Claude-token guard) is exploratory, outside every Holm family, descriptive. All use the same models, split and train-only rule; the prompt-only E30-GEPA cell stays in family E. A competitor that does not finish leaves the family with the reason recorded.

**5-AMENDMENT 9 ERRATUM 7: "no retry credit" sensitivity (2026-10-12 12:59 UTC; before any final score; no held-out score exists).** The primary analysis stays exactly as registered in 5O-1: a cell that is an infrastructure error on its first attempt and succeeds on a retry is scored from the retry, and an `infra_final` cell after 3 retries scores 0. Added sensitivity analysis, "no retry credit": any cell whose FIRST attempt was an infrastructure error scores 0 (final-eval-eng's `results_long_first_attempt_infra_zero.csv`, 8c9c6da5a). It is reported beside the primary for every contrast that involves an artifact on the `flagged_above_seed` list of `infra_diagnostic.json` (first-attempt or `infra_final` infrastructure rate of at least 5% with its Wilson lower bound above the seed's Wilson upper bound in the same window, 5O-4). If primary and sensitivity disagree both are shown and the primary stands. Rationale: the metering audit (1f94da247) found tau2 INFRASTRUCTURE_ERROR terminations that are candidate-induced and follow write-tool calls, so retried infra errors may favour such artifacts. The primary is not switched because that evidence comes from search-time data; the sensitivity analysis bounds how much retry credit could matter.

### 5-AMENDMENT 11: CAPEVOLVE v2 RERUNS (2026-10-13 07:04 UTC; before any v2 run; no v1 final (held-out) score had been seen when 11 was written)

Source: `improve/V2_PLAN.md` and the user's v2 decisions in `decisions.md` (2026-10-12); machine-readable copy `experiments/configs/v2_registration.json`. Registered before the v1 final window results are read, and before the main commit SHA exists; the SHA is registered later as an 11E version line, before the first v2 test rollout.
**11A. Framing.** v2 is post-hoc method development done after the v1 search-time results and after the v1 diagnoses of the gate and of agent-optimize. It is its own pre-registered family, reported beside v1 and never replacing it. Amendments 1 to 10 are unchanged; v1 results stay as registered.
**11B. Arms, suites, budgets.** HC-2 (`hc2: true`, `promotion_rule P2`, `split_mode pooled`) and AO-2 (`ao_engine conductor`, P2, pooled, single objective only), both with code helpers where the surface allows. Suite A: SpreadsheetBench, Gemma-4-31B, surface [system-prompt, skill-package] with helper scripts. Suite D: tau2 retail, gpt-4.1-mini on Azure (policy + tools). Suite E: tau2 airline, gpt-4.1-mini (policy + tools). Suite B is excluded because its GPT-5.5 judge is capped (a capacity reason, not an outcome). 3 seeds per arm and suite; rollout budgets as in v1 per suite; optimizer Sonnet 5.5 capped at 4M tokens per search (AO-2 soft cap 1.5M, so optimizer compute differs between the two arms and is reported, not matched); target models and simulators unchanged.
**11C. Data.** Pool = train U val (A: train-80 U val-40 = 120; D: opt-49 U sel-25 = 74; E: opt-20 U sel-10 = 30), partitioned once (seeded, stratified, sealed) into feedback F (about 50%) and confirm C (about 50%). The proposer sees F only; C yields accept/reject bits only. The champion is chosen by P2 and the internal C estimate (champion vs seed on C, 4 fresh trials per side); that estimate is a search-time, selected-on diagnostic and never a result. Held-out sets: A the same frozen T = 70 draw as v1; D the sealed test-40; E the official test-20 (development-exposed). All three were or will be scored once for v1, so v2 is a SECOND use of the same sets, labelled with the reuse count; no v2 design decision has used any of them.
**11D. Test rule.** Each frozen v2 version is scored ONCE on its suite's held-out set. Iteration to v2.1 and later uses only internal C estimates. No v2 design, freeze or launch decision may use any v1 or v2 held-out score. Every version scored on test is reported. The primary version per suite is the FIRST frozen version (v2.0); later versions are secondary, with their own Holm over the versions scored, so there is no selective reporting and no credit for picking the best version.
**11E. Version freeze.** A version = (code commit SHA, spec/config, prompts, helper files), hashed in the registry before its test scoring; its registry line precedes the first test rollout of that version. The pinned main commit SHA is registered as the first 11E line when it exists; any code or spec change defines a new version.
**11F. Scoring window.** v1 and v2 are scored in different windows. In each suite the v2 window is one interleaved randomized window (amendment 2, 5H to 5I) that re-scores, inside it, the seed artifact (A n = 2, D and E n = 10) and the v1 GEPA champions (the comparator pre-specified in 11G, chosen before the v2 window); v2 contrasts use ONLY these in-window anchors. v1-window numbers are shown beside them and not pooled. The seed drift rule of 5I applies (block-adjusted headline if the seed drifts by more than 2 SE).
**11G. Family V2 (confirmatory within v2; Holm within suite, m = 4; paired, task = cluster, artifact-level CR1 and pipeline-level t(S-1) as in 5M-2).** HC-2 vs seed; AO-2 vs seed; HC-2 vs v1 GEPA; AO-2 vs v1 GEPA. The comparator is fixed now because GEPA is the one competitor run on every suite: A E10 gepa, D E20-GEPA, E E30-GEPA. Descriptive only (CI, no p, no Holm): v2 vs the best v1 competitor by test mean and vs every other v1 competitor (both data-dependent, flagged as such), v2 vs the v1 stock-gate and rule-P hill-climb, HC-2 vs AO-2. No equivalence claims. The cross-suite rule 9B, the inert rule 9D, daggers (AO-2 gold visibility is re-checked per suite), the 5O infra rule with the no-retry-credit sensitivity, 9H, the counted-rollout definition and the reporting columns of 5M-4 carry over.
**11H. Planning MDE (conditional on the 3 champions, tau 0.15, S = 3; final n as in v1: A n = 1 per arm and n = 2 for the seed at T = 70, D n = 5 and seed n = 10 at T = 40, E n = 5 and seed n = 10 at T = 20; 80% power).** v2 vs seed: A 0.106, D 0.093, E 0.111 (Holm m = 4: 0.128, 0.112, 0.136). v2 vs v1 GEPA (both arms at n as above): A 0.119, D 0.102, E 0.119 (m = 4: 0.143, 0.124, 0.146). Effects below these are inconclusive.
**11I. Reported for every arm:** cost (USD, optimizer tokens, rollouts), wall time, infrastructure rate with Wilson interval against the seed in the same window, promotions per search, inert fraction, per-task determinism variance (reward variance across trials, seed vs champion) and selection-to-final shrinkage (5P-2: internal C estimate vs held-out).

**5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 1: v2.0 final version (2026-10-13 13:14 UTC; before any v2 search; no v2 search has run, smokes only, discarded).** Supersedes the code pins of 11E (6b94ccba0, then 6131641e6) and the P2 defaults and Suite E scaling rule of `v2_version_2_0.json`. Code: main `a3f5eeb6d54d9782c9fb3985ca1d63560f6cc4bb` = 6131641e6 plus #832 (`hc2_final_estimate_trials`), #833 (`ao_final_estimate_trials`) and #834 (`finalize:false`; helper surface ignores files byte-identical to the seed). Reason: the smokes showed the default P2 sizes fund zero rounds within v1's budgets. Budget-fit rule (`improve/p2_budget_fit.md`, calibrated with the null-pool simulator at per-task heterogeneity SD 0, 0.1, 0.2): K = 4, one survivor, kill-only rungs, no in-search final estimate; acceptance by paired confirmation on C at one-sided alpha 0.12 (t_crit about 1.19 to 1.23) with mean >= 0.02. Per-suite settings are in `experiments/configs/v2_version_2_0.json` (A: 600 calls, rungs [8,24], incumbent trials 2, conf 116, ctrl 1, z 1.187; D: 550, [8,24], 3, 72, 2, 1.195; E: 300, [8,16], 2, 42, 3, 1.231). Both arms, all suites: finalize false, both final-estimate reserves 0, `ao_k_prop` 4. Approximation, stated: the code's confirmation statistic uses the heterogeneity-aware SE (the larger of the model SE and the spread of per-task deltas) and is applied as z >= `p2_z_conf`, which approximates the simulated cluster-robust t. Registered caveats: planned power over two rounds for a true +0.10 is 0.36 to 0.50 (A), 0.38 to 0.54 (D), 0.25 to 0.38 (E), and for +0.20 it is 0.84 to 0.92, 0.86 to 0.94 and 0.69 to 0.86; a null result at these budgets is uninformative; the family-wise false-promotion rate is at most 0.10 per round and higher per search across rounds. Outside the search algorithm: the v2 tau adapters (copied from v1) add a read-only `reference/` guard (hashes from tau-runner) and `admit_v2` uses a 30 s V2-only queue rule.

**5-AMENDMENT 10/11 ADDENDUM: tau2 tool-consistency check penalises tool-output changes (2026-10-13 20:31 UTC; descriptive analysis note; changes no rule; before any Suite D/E held-out score).** Source: final-eval-eng, `audit/silent_env_failures.md` and `experiments/final/infra_mechanism.py` (0b2b7077a). tau2's tool-result consistency check compares the live tool output with a pristine replay and labels any mismatch INFRASTRUCTURE_ERROR after 4 attempts. With an editable tools surface, any change to a tool's output trips it: refusals on Suite E, reworded errors or changed return text on Suites B, C and D. Under 5O those cells score 0 (`infra_final` = failure; the no-retry-credit sensitivity of amendment 9 erratum 7 scores them 0 as well).
(i) *v1 consequence.* Tools-surface arms are systematically penalised relative to prompt-only arms (P, GEPA policy-only). Every tools-vs-policy decomposition (PT / P / T, the E25 sub-artifacts) must report this, with the primary and the sensitivity analysis side by side and the per-artifact mismatch sub-types (refusal, reworded error, changed return).
(ii) *v2 D/E consequence.* At search time these cells are treated as infrastructure (missing, retried, quarantined), not as candidate faults, so the P2 gate does not see a penalty that the final window applies. A search-versus-held-out gap is expected for tools edits; it is reported per artifact (selection-to-final shrinkage 5P-2, split by tools-edited vs not).
(iii) *No code change for v2.0.* A possible v2.1 rule, 'a tool-consistency mismatch caused by a changed tool counts as a candidate fault at search time', would be a new registered version (11E), decided on internal C data only (11D).

Every other hold is unchanged (outcome-based V2 infrastructure rate > 5%, V2 queue >= 30 s, v1 30-minute queue > 20 s, v1 rate < 85% with queue > 15 s). The rule reads gateway tags and call counts, never rewards.

**5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 11: v2 scoring-window clarifications (2026-10-13 21:34 UTC; specification of 11F and 11G, made before any v1 or v2 held-out score was computed; no score of any v1 GEPA artifact has been looked at).** Questions from final-eval-eng, ruled by the statistics reviewer.
(1) *Seed-check cell.* The in-window 'seed-check' cell of 9D stays in every v2 window (one extra labelled copy of the seed artifact at the arms' n); it feeds no test.
(2) *v1 GEPA anchor (the comparator of 11G).* The anchor is the GEPA artifact that received the same search budget as the v2 arms: D the E20-GEPA and E the E30-GEPA final-state artifacts (550 and 300 counted rollouts, the v2 budgets), and for Suite A the E10 GEPA artifact at 600 counted rollouts (the v2 Suite A budget; if a GEPA seed ended before 600 its native end-of-search artifact). The shorter matched-checkpoint artifact of v1 (N*) is NOT the anchor: it has less budget than the v2 arms. It may appear as a descriptive row if it is already scored in a v1 window (not re-scored). The choice is fixed here by budget parity, before any held-out GEPA score is computed or looked at. Anchors are re-scored in the v2 window at the arms' n (A n = 1, D and E n = 5), three seeds each.
(3) *v2 champion.* The final state of the search (the last accepted artifact, else the seed), no selection among versions or among candidates. An inert v2 champion (tree hash equal to the seed's) is flagged and, as in 9D, mapped to the seed-check cell with primary delta 0; no separate rescoring cell is scheduled for it.
(4) *Order seed.* `ORDER_SEED` 20261013 with the suite-specific stream is accepted; the order file is hashed in the registry before the first rollout of each suite.
(5) *Seed drift rule.* The 5I check uses only the in-window seed scores across blocks (task-demeaned); it needs no v1-window mean. The v1 seed means are NOT computed before the v2 windows; they are shown beside the v2 results afterwards, descriptively.
(6) *Comparison-family file.* The confirmatory rows are exactly the four of 11G per suite (m = 4). Descriptive rows may be listed in the same file, flagged descriptive: HC-2 vs AO-2, each v2 arm vs the v1 stock-gate and rule-P hill-climb, each v2 arm vs every other v1 competitor and the best-by-test-mean competitor; the latter rows use v1-window numbers (no in-window anchor) and carry a window-shift note (the seed's in-window v2 mean against its v1-window mean).

**5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 12: scorers of the v2 windows (2026-10-13 21:42 UTC; record of a scorer fact from final-eval-eng, decisions.md d03291d26; before any v2 held-out score).** The Suite A v2 window scores all cells, anchors included, with main's SpreadsheetBench adapter at `a3f5eeb6d` (sha256 `2036bc67…`, the helpers carrier) and the v2 `code_exec_docker` files (`api.py` `ca25a43c…`, KERNEL_TIMEOUT 10 minutes), not with the v1 final scorer (patched adapter `b128a098…`, `api.py` `50b1ba48…`, KERNEL_TIMEOUT 10800 s); Suites D and E use the V2 adapter copies (retail `eb809461…`, airline `26773c88…`) rather than the v1 scorers (`b983cd26…`, `fbf0ca97…`). Consequences stated in advance: (a) every v2 contrast compares cells scored by the SAME scorer in the SAME window (arms, anchors, seed, seed-check), so the confirmatory rows of 11G are internally consistent; (b) v1-window numbers are shown beside and never pooled (as in 11F), and any descriptive row that uses v1-window numbers carries the scorer difference together with the window-shift note; (c) a score-equivalence check of the v2 scorers against the v1 scorers on recorded search-split outputs (reward and feedback compared, no held-out data) is requested from final-eval-eng and its result (mismatch count, and for Suite A the effect of the 10-minute vs 3-hour kernel timeout) is registered before the descriptive cross-window rows are reported.

**5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 14: v2.1-AO2, a secondary AO-2 version (2026-10-13 23:12 UTC; 11E version line; before any v2.1 run and before any v2 held-out score).** AO-2 E seeds 0 and 1 ended by the core's default plateau rule (11 proposals without a champion change at 41% of the rollout budget; `policy.py` 211 to 215), leaving about 59% of the rollout budget unused; the registered v2.0 AO-2 spec left `ao_plateau_*` at the defaults. v2.0 AO-2 stays the PRIMARY AO-2 version, unchanged: all v2.0 AO-2 seeds run to their own registered end and are reported as such, including early plateau stops. v2.1-AO2 is a SECONDARY version (11D): identical to v2.0 AO-2 (same code `d68775bbd`, no code change) except `ao_plateau_floor` 0.8 instead of 0.4 (no plateau stop before 80% of the rollout budget is spent; `ao_plateau_n` and every other knob unchanged). **5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 16: optimizer exposure to confirm-split evaluate events in the V2 D/E sandboxes (2026-10-13 23:19 UTC; disclosure and isolation fix, no analysis rule changes; before any V2 held-out score).** tau-runner found that in the V2 Suite D and E sandboxes `<W>/.capevolve/run_main/events.jsonl` was readable by the optimizer and carries the P2 confirm-chunk evaluate events (reward mean, stderr, n_subset) as ordinary events, i.e. aggregate feedback about the confirm stratum C that the design (11C) hides from the proposer. **5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 29: post-promotion top-up not priced in the budget-fit planning, Suite A HC-2 (2026-10-14 03:54 UTC; descriptive record from search-time counts; no scores used; source capevolve-runner, computed with the run's own code: `RunDir.remaining_metric_calls`, `eval_index.missing`, `P2Params.worst_case_cost`).** The registered budget-fit plan (erratum 1 of amendment 11) funds 'two rounds' as baseline 124 + 2 x 238 = 600 on Suite A and assumed no top-up of a new incumbent. After a promotion, `plan_round` adds the new incumbent's F top-up (`p2_incumbent_trials` 2 on every F task, |F| = 62, i.e. 124 F draws) to the worst-case cost of the next round. HC-2 seed 0: stopped at spent 362 of cap 600 (remaining 238) after round r000 promoted cand_0004; cand_0004 had 24 of the 124 F draws, so the top-up is 100; K=1 costs 198 + 100 = 298 > 238 and K=4 costs 238 + 100 = 338 > 238, hence `budget_unfunded` (internal estimate disabled). HC-2 seed 1: stopped at spent 362 of 600 (remaining 238) after r000 promoted cand_0001, which had 0 F draws under its capability hash, so the top-up is 124; K=1: 198 + 124 = 322 > 238; K=4: 238 + 124 = 362 > 238; unfunded. Worst-case round components with |C| = 58: K=1: 8 + 16 + 58 + 116 = 198; K=4: 32 + 32 + 58 + 116 = 238. HC-2 seed 2 (still running at the time of writing): the incumbent is the seed (topped up at the baseline, top-up 0), 3 rounds opened (r000 and r001 with every candidate killed at the rungs, r002 with K shrunk to 2 at 135 remaining, cand_0009 in its C confirmation). Consequence for the planned-power statements of erratum 1: a Suite A seed that promotes at round 1 gets one round, not two, so the 'over two rounds' planned power (+0.10: 0.36 to 0.50; +0.20: 0.84 to 0.92) applies to a seed that does not promote in round 1; for a seed that does, the per-round power of `improve/p2_budget_fit.md` (about 0.20 to 0.29 per round at +0.10 on A) is the relevant figure for any further improvement, and a stopped seed is not evidence of a null result. The registered rule is not changed. AO-2 and v2.1-AO2 are not affected by this mechanism.

**5-AMENDMENT 11 ERRATUM 30: Suites B and C transfer extras, exploratory (2026-10-14 04:30 UTC; registered before any transfer job runs; coordinator decisions 10-11 (b); orders registered by `freeze.py --suites A,B,C,D,E`, registry line 04eef6f83).** Orders `order_B_transfer.jsonl` (sha256 `d98f6455357c…`, 1,920 jobs: hill-climb, agent-optimize, GEPA, one-shot and stock-gate hill-climb x 3 seeds plus the seed artifact, i.e. 16 cells x 40 test tasks x 3 trials) and `order_C_transfer.jsonl` (sha256 `ba250077096b…`, 960 jobs, 16 cells x 20 test tasks x 3 trials); registry items `order_B_transfer`, `order_C_transfer`; checked by this reviewer: the hashes equal the files on disk, every job is on the test split with target, user simulator and NL judge all Azure gpt-4.1 via :4112, tags `FINAL-<S>-xfer-*`. Status: EXPLORATORY, outside every Holm family, no p-values in any headline, descriptive CIs only. Stated confounds: the user simulator and the judge change together with the target (gpt-oss to gpt-4.1), so a transfer effect is not attributable to the target alone; the seed artifact is a cell in both orders, so every arm's transfer effect is a within-configuration contrast against the seed. Test-set reuse: B's sealed test-40 is the same task set as Suite D's test-40, and C's test-20 is the same as Suite E's; the transfer scoring adds a further use of each, counted in the reuse tally. Inert artifacts (tree hash equal to the seed's) are scheduled as ordinary cells in these orders (not mapped to a seed-check cell as in 9D); they act as extra repeats of the seed and are labelled as such, and any contrast involving them follows 9D (primary delta 0, rescored values a measurement check). As for every held-out window, the results files are hash-registered before use and nothing is analysed before the v2 freeze (erratum 28).

Its contrasts in family V2 (AO-2 vs seed, AO-2 vs GEPA) stay as registered and read "AO-2 returned its v2 starting artifact (no promotion)"; the AO-2 vs seed delta is the effect of the v2 start (helper carrier text plus empty package), not a search effect. (4) Search effect of HC-2 is shown descriptively as HC-2 vs AO-2 (the v2 start held fixed), no Holm. (5) No extra v2-seed cell is needed (the AO-2 cells are it). (6) This choice is fixed by the wording of 11F and by what the builder produced, not by any outcome; it does not use any Suite A v2 score. The order file is hash-registered before the first rollout.
