**5-AMENDMENT 12 ERRATUM 70: user decision of 2026-10-25 to remove the Claude Code one-shot arm from the paper (logged as the user's decision, relayed by team-lead; no registry number changes).** The one-shot arm (NC-1SHOT) is removed from the paper's text, tables and figures. The BRIDGE1 window and all its numbers (erratum 67 and 68) and the ABL-W2 score of NC-1SHOT stay in the registry unchanged. *Reviewer's risk note, for the file:* the one-shot contrast (+0.025 against search 1, 95% CI [-0.099, 0.149], raw p 0.678, in the same session; +0.138 against its seed) was the only direct evidence in the study on whether the search loop adds held-out gain over a single informed edit from the seed's traces. Its interval is too wide to say the loop adds nothing (the detectable difference is about 0.16 for two single champions), and too wide to say it adds something; it was a single champion in one Claude Code session. With the arm removed, the paper holds NO evidence for the claim that the loop itself, with its evaluations and gate, produces the gain, and the five remaining ablations (all designed after the main results, each against search 1) do not bear on it except for naive Claude Code (which is a different loop). Therefore the paper must not claim that the loop or the evaluations produce the gain, nor that a single edit would not match it; statements about what the proposer sees or may edit stay as the ablations support them (exploratory, raw p, one seed). The team-lead has told mlsys-lead to add no such claim; the reviewer will check the next push for any sentence that attributes the gain to the loop, the evaluations or the gate as a cause.

**5-AMENDMENT 12 ERRATUM 71: K02 search stopped early, champion rule, route change for the K02 window, and the conditional follow-up (user decisions of 2026-10-26 relayed by team-lead; registered before any K02 held-out score exists; exploratory).** (1) *Early stop.* The user stopped the K02 search at 17 scored candidates and 2,700 of the 3,000 rollouts, because the upstream litellm route to gpt-oss has returned 429s since 17:50 UTC. This DEVIATES from addendum 16, which fixed the champion at the end of the search. The reviewer read `events.jsonl` from disk: 17 candidates scored (cand_0001 to cand_0017), accepted: cand_0001 (+0.047, SE 0.056), cand_0002 (+0.073, 0.055), cand_0004 (+0.120, 0.051) and cand_0011 (+0.040, 0.041); cand_0017 was rejected, and nothing after cand_0011 was accepted. The champion by the registered rule (last accepted candidate) is therefore cand_0011, unchanged by stopping after cand_0017; the stop does not change which candidate is the champion, but it changes how many candidates were screened (17 against the planned 19) and K02 is not a full-budget search; it must be called a search stopped at 2,700 rollouts. The driver was still running when the reviewer read the log; no scoring of any candidate on test ids has occurred. (2) *Route change.* The K02 window runs the agent and the user simulator on RITS `openai/gpt-oss-120b-a100` for EVERY cell (the K02 champion, the seed at n = 20, the seed-check and K2 search 1), instead of the litellm `aws/gpt-oss-120b` route used in all earlier windows. It is the same open model on a different serving stack, and the earlier K2 search 1 value (0.630 in ABL-W1 and MERGED, 0.585 in BRIDGE1) came from the aws route. *Consequences:* all K02 contrasts are WITHIN the new session, as before, and K2 search 1's value in this window, not 0.630, is the comparison reference; the level of any cell (seed, K2 search 1) may differ from earlier windows because of the route, and the window check (K2 search 1 minus seed; earlier windows +0.145, +0.130, +0.130, BRIDGE1 +0.113) is read with that in mind. The search itself ran the agent on the aws route, so the K02 champion is evaluated on a different stack from the one it was selected on, which can only lower its score relative to the in-window seed if the stacks differ in ways the edits depend on. The scorer pins other than the route (adapter `9c797d8a`, judge route, rule 5a, infra retries) are to be stated in ablation-lead's order and checked before the ack. (3) *Conditional follow-up.* The user decided: if the K02 champion's held-out mean in this window is at least 0.63, five new searches follow (three seeds at k = 0.2, GATE-NAIVE: accept if the mean paired difference is at least 0, and GATE-NONE: accept every candidate), all on RITS gpt-oss-a100 with the Sonnet optimizer. *Quantity the 0.63 threshold applies to:* the K02 champion's held-out mean over the 20 test ids at k = 10 in the K02 window, as reported by the registered `k02_analysis.py` (the ARM MEAN, not a gain). Reviewer's notes on the rule: (a) 0.63 is K2's earlier result on a different route; it is a DECISION threshold for running more searches, not a test, and it enters no inference; (b) the mean of one champion over 20 tasks has a standard error of about 0.045 (a within-variance of 0.1236 over 20 tasks at 10 trials), so a champion whose true level is 0.60 clears 0.63 about one time in four, and one at 0.66 fails about one in four; (c) the K02 gain over the in-window seed and over K2 search 1 in the same session are reported beside the mean, and if the in-window seed level differs from 0.472 to 0.500 by more than 0.05 the reviewer will say so, because then 0.63 in this window is not the same bar as 0.63 earlier; (d) the five new searches get their own registered windows, their own order and an exploratory label, and are not pooled with K2 or K02 without a new addendum. The reviewer reports the K02 held-out mean to team-lead the moment the analysis has run.

The contrast with K2 search 1 remains a contrast of gate PLUS serving stack. **5-AMENDMENT 12 ADDENDUM 21 AND ERRATUM 90: post hoc re-analysis of scored data (part 0) and the K30 window (parts 1 to 3) (2026-10-27; requested by team-lead on user approval; registered before any K30 rollout and before the post hoc script has been run on the real data).** The planning document (mlsys-lead, `analysis/inputs/power_analysis_20261027.md`, registered as a planning input and not as an analysis) estimated that more trials per cell cannot settle the close comparators on tau2 (trial noise is 20 to 38 percent of the variance of the comparison; task and search heterogeneity dominates) and can settle the gate contrast and tighten the ablations. The reviewer did not re-derive its simulations; the reviewer recomputed the single-champion detectable difference from the registered formula (tau 0.15, tau_int 0.10, within-variance 0.1236, 20 tasks): 0.158 at 10 trials and 0.133 at 30 trials for one comparison, 0.149 at m = 2 and 0.168 at m = 5 at 30 trials; for the mean of three searches against the seed 0.126 at 10 trials (seed 20) and 0.117 at 30.

**PART 0: POST HOC RE-ANALYSIS (no new data; labelled post hoc here and, by this addendum, in the paper).** The methods below were chosen after the registered results (Holm, m = 10; AppWorld Holm over the 78 pairs of a session) were seen, and they are chosen in the direction that rejects more. The registered results do not change. Script `posthoc_reanalysis.py` (registered by hash now; a first run on the real data, to test the script, printed the numbers below and nothing else was done with them; they are given here so that they are on record before any further use). (A) tau2, MERGED, CapEvolve (searches 1, 2, 3) minus each of ten comparators, task-paired: the registered Holm (m = 10) passes HarnessLens and Meta-Harness (0.037 each); an exact sign-flip permutation maxT (2^20 sign vectors, studentised, one vector for all ten comparisons) gives adjusted p of HarnessLens 0.011, Meta-Harness 0.012, GEPA 0.025, MIPROv2 0.026, WikiSkill 0.041 (five below 0.05), ACE 0.120, GEPA (full) 0.123, EvoSkill 0.146, SkillOpt 0.188, Trace2Skill 0.206. The method flips the signs of the 20 task differences, so it assumes symmetry about 0 under the null and resamples tasks, not searches; it treats the three searches as one fixed artifact. A bootstrap maxT (the lead's note: 0.052 to 0.27) is not robust at 20 tasks and is not registered. (B) pooled test: CapEvolve minus the equal-weight mean of the nine non-inert comparators +0.091 [0.025, 0.158], p 0.0096 (CR1 t(19)); with HarnessLens as a tenth comparator +0.095 [0.029, 0.161], p 0.0074. HarnessLens, whose champion is the unmodified agent, is excluded from the primary of this part because its mean is the baseline. The pooled test asks whether CapEvolve is above the AVERAGE comparator, not above each; it is not a replacement for the registered family. (C) AppWorld one session (test_normal, 5 trials per task, 168 tasks): Holm over CapEvolve's ten comparisons, computed from the PUBLISHED raw p to four decimals (transcribed by mlsys-lead from the issue comments; the reviewer has no per-task AppWorld data, cannot check the transcription against the source, and cannot run a pooled test or permutation test there): Meta-Harness and HarnessLens (published p 0.0000) pass; MIPROv2 0.011 and WikiSkill 0.046 pass; EvoSkill 0.065, GEPA 0.190, SkillOpt 0.439, ACE 0.439, GEPA (full) 0.804, Trace2Skill 0.811 do not: four of ten against two under the session's 78-pair Holm. The WikiSkill value (0.046) lies near 0.05 and depends on a p rounded to four decimals. *Rules for the paper:* (P0-1) every number of part 0 is labelled POST HOC wherever it appears, with the registered result beside it; (P0-2) the registered Holm (m = 10) stays the headline of the tau2 comparison (two of ten) and the permutation result is given as a sensitivity in the statistics appendix, with its assumption; (P0-3) the abstract and the body must not say "CapEvolve is significantly better than five comparators" on the strength of the maxT; (P0-4) the AppWorld family choice (ten comparisons of CapEvolve instead of 78 pairs) is stated with the reason (the claim concerns CapEvolve against each comparator) and as post hoc; (P0-5) the pooled test is described as a test against the average comparator; (P0-6) multiplicity: parts A, B and C are three re-analyses of data already seen and are not a family.

**PARTS 1 TO 3: THE K30 WINDOW (exploratory; one fresh session).** *Design (the lead's proposal, fixed):* tau2 airline, the 20 test tasks, the aws route (`openai/aws/gpt-oss-120b` for agent, user simulator and judge), 48 workers, adapter `9c797d8a`, `max_infra_retries` 30, rule 5a, all cells at 30 trials per task, in ONE interleaved randomised order. *Cells (frozen artifacts, registered copies; the order must name them exactly):* GATE-NAIVE-K2 (`bf4d5ba2`), GATE-NONE-K2 (`861e361e`); CapEvolve searches 1, 2, 3 as `GATE-K2-s0` (`de4ed1c4`), `GATE-K2-s1` (`89b66636`), `GATE-K2-s3` (`d532256a`) with the MERGED manifest trees; the five ablation arms as in MERGED: `V2-A3a` trace and reward (`998cc728`), `V2-A3b` grader output only (`ea969859`), `V2-A2a` tools only (`fbcfd1c0`), `V2-A2b` policy only (`696279f1`), `V2-A1a` naive Claude Code (`510895f8`); the seed at 30 trials; the seed-check at 30 trials. That is 10 champion cells x 600 jobs + seed 600 + seed-check 600 = 7,200 jobs (the lead's figure of about 6,800 omits the seed-check at 30; with the seed-check at 10 it is 6,800 and the seed-check is then a different precision from the seed; the reviewer registers it at 30, so seed-check minus seed has the same precision as the other contrasts; the lead may choose 10 and tell the reviewer before the order is built). K2 seed 2 and NC-LOOP are not in the window. *Trial seeds:* the order's `trial_seed` is a function of (suite, task, trial, extra salt) only (`order.py` `trial_seed`), so every tau2-airline window so far used the SAME 20 x 20 task-trial seeds (the reviewer verified: the seed values of K2 search 1 are identical across K2GATEWIN, K02WIN, BRIDGE1 and MERGED, 200 of 200, and the 790 distinct seeds over all tau2 airline orders reflect that); trials 0 to 29 of K30 would repeat the seeds of trials 0 to 19 of earlier windows for the same task. The lead's requirement is that K30 trial seeds be disjoint from all earlier windows: this needs a NEW salt (the `--extra` argument or an added `--seed-salt`), which changes the builder; the reviewer requires a builder variant `k30_order.py` (v3_order.py/order.py with ONE change, the salt string for trial_seed), registered by hash before the order, and the order's meta must record the salt. A trial seed fixes the simulated user's randomness, so seed reuse across windows would make the earlier and later trials of the same artifact correlated; with a new salt K30 is independent. *Comparator table: option (a)* (the reviewer registers the lead's preference): K30 supports ONLY within-window contrasts (gate, ablations, CapEvolve against the seed). The comparator table of the paper (CapEvolve against ten comparators, MERGED, 10 trials) is NOT re-estimated, and no cross-session pooled estimate (option (b)) is formed, because the artifact-level shift between sessions is not common (earlier windows: K2 search 1 minus seed +0.145, +0.130, +0.130, +0.113, +0.153, +0.185; the seed itself moved from 0.470 to 0.528; the session effect is not estimable from a comparator's single session). The analysis script prints a session-shift check (K30 minus MERGED for every artifact scored in both) that feeds no test: if the shifts agree it is evidence for a common shift and a LATER addendum may consider pooling; if they do not, the paper says the contrasts are within-session only. *Primary contrasts* (task-paired, artifact-level CR1 t(19), raw p, Holm within the family): GATE family (m = 2): NAIVE minus search 1 and NONE minus search 1; ABLATION family (m = 5): each ablation arm minus search 1; CapEvolve (m = 1): the mean of searches 1 to 3 minus the in-window seed (artifact-level, and pipeline-level t(2) with the between-search spread). A joint Holm over all seven contrasts against search 1 is printed as a sensitivity. Secondary, raw p, no family: each gate and ablation arm minus the seed; search 2 and 3 minus search 1; NAIVE minus NONE; seed-check minus seed; the 5I drift check (the window should last about 3 hours, so the hour blocks are assessable). Script `k30_analysis.py` (registered; tested on synthetic stand-ins only). *What this window can and cannot do (reviewer's reading of the planning document):* it reduces the trial-noise term; the planning document estimates that this settles the NONE contrast (almost all trial noise) and moves NAIVE and the component arms to raw p near 0.04 to 0.1 with none surviving a 5-way correction; the search-level and task-level variance of single-search arms does not shrink with trials. A significant K30 contrast for a gate or ablation arm is a statement about ONE search of that arm and the 20 tasks, not about the gate or the component in general. The confounds of erratum 82 (override, switch commit, load, one search per arm, K2 search 1 a single past search) apply to the gate contrasts; the ablation arms' confounds are those of addendum 11. The window result may be published together with the three means of searches 1 to 3 at 30 trials; it must not be pooled with the 10-trial results of the same artifacts as if independent, because the artifacts and tasks are the same. *Order and ack:* ablation-lead builds the order with the new salt and sends the builder, order, manifest and champions file, plus a statement that every champion's frozen files equal the registered copies (hash of the tree against the list above); the reviewer hashes, checks the trial-seed disjointness against all earlier orders (the reviewer's script), and acks before any rollout. After the window the reviewer registers the results hashes and runs `k30_analysis.py OUT ROOT MERGED_ROOT` unchanged.

## 6. Missingness and invalid rollouts
The harness averages only valid trials (harness.py:587-601), drops a task from a paired vector when either side has zero valid trials (harness.py:1060-1065), and the coverage guard (harness.py:3022, gate.py:397) looks only at the candidate side and whole tasks. Simulation (sim_footprint.md d): dropped candidate-induced errors turn a null edit into 52-89% acceptance while coverage stays >=0.96.
1. Classification by a frozen deterministic classifier (error class, exit code, exception-type allowlist), applied blind to arm and reward, published with the code. Anything unclassified counts as a failure.
2. Exogenous failures (provider 429/5xx, gateway timeout with provider-side evidence, container pull failure, host OOM not attributable to the artifact): retried up to R=3 further attempts, same policy and cap on EVERY arm; retries triggered by error class only, never by a prior outcome; cost charged to the arm. A trial exhausting R is a missing cell.
3. Artifact-induced failures (exception in artifact code, malformed/missing output, tool crash caused by the artifact, exceeding per-task wall-clock/turn/token cap) count as a trial with reward 0; never dropped from the task mean.
4. Complete-case primary analysis over tasks with >=1 valid trial in BOTH arms only if (a) missing fraction <=5% per arm and (b) missing counts not associated with arm (Fisher exact p>=.10). Otherwise report the contrast with bounds only.
5. Bounds always shown next to the primary estimate: pessimistic = missing cells of the evaluated arm scored 0 and of the comparator arm scored 1; optimistic = the reverse. A contrast is "robust" only if the pessimistic-bound CI excludes 0. Wide bounds are shown, not hidden.
6. Inner-loop min_coverage=0.6 may stay as a search-time safeguard but computed on the paired intersection, not the candidate side only. Confirmatory evaluation requires >=95% of (arm, task) cells valid after retries; otherwise re-execute the suite or declare it invalid for the headline.
7. Per-arm report: attempted, retried, exogenous-missing, artifact-failed counts by class.

## 7. Minimum detectable effect and budget planning
Model (sim_mde.py; Monte Carlo check var .000530 vs formula .000544): Var(theta_hat) = DEFF*tau^2/T + sigma_s^2/S + DEFF*(tau_int^2 + noise/n)/(T*S); alpha .05 two-sided, power .80. [PLACEHOLDER] inputs until the pilot reports them: tau=0.15 (SD across tasks of true per-task advantage), tau_int=0.10 (seed x task interaction), sigma_s=0.03 (SD of champion quality across seeds), trial noise 0.297/n (mu .55, kappa 1.5), family size 5 with ICC .10 (DEFF 1.4). Sensitivity in sim_mde.md.

MDE in absolute reward points, S=3:

| T | n | artifact-level (the 3 champions) | Holm m=3 | pipeline-level t(2) | Holm m=3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 40 | 3 | .127 | .147 | .261 | .423 |
| 40 | 5 | .112 | .129 | .234 | .379 |
| 100 | 3 | .080 | .093 | .180 | .292 |
| 100 | 5 | .071 | .082 | .164 | .267 |
| 280 | 3 | .048 | .055 | .131 | .212 |
| 280 | 5 | .042 | .049 | .123 | .200 |

At n=5 pipeline-level MDE is .142/.098/.071 at S=5 and .105/.070/.048 at S=10 for T=40/100/280. Method-vs-method (tau .10, artifact-level, n=5, S=3): .095/.060/.036.

Reading: (1) with 3 seeds no pipeline-level claim below ~12 points is powered at any plausible T (t(2)=4.30 is the binding constraint). Either claim only the artifact-level estimand and say so, or buy seeds (S=5 roughly halves pipeline MDE) at smaller per-search budget. (2) Method-vs-method gaps of 2-5 points are undetectable at T<=100; descriptive unless T>=280 and artifact-level estimand. (3) Final-eval rollouts per suite = (4 methods x S + C controls) x T x n; S=3, C=2: 1,680 (T40,n3), 4,200 (T100,n3), 11,760 (T280,n3). campaign.yaml caps the WHOLE campaign at 10,000 target rollouts; two suites at T=100,n=3 use 8,400 (84%) before any search rollouts. Coordinator should raise the cap, cut arms (one shared baseline; one-shot repair one seed only), cut n to 2 on the final set, or accept the left-hand MDE columns.

## 8. Promotion and selection rules feeding the confirmatory analysis
Search-time gates are heuristics; confirmatory numbers come only from the untouched final set. Every method uses the SAME promotion rule P:
1. Screen on dev-val with the paired gate k_se=1 on the full UNMASKED delta vector (footprint_gate=False).
2. Confirm: re-evaluate candidate and incumbent with fresh trials (new seeds, n_conf>=10/task) on the same dev-val tasks. Promote iff mean delta >=0.02 and delta/SE_paired >=2.0 (z=1.645 instead raises null promotion from 3.7% to 7.7% per 10-iteration search).
3. The incumbent's confirmation measurement is the baseline for the next iteration.
4. Per-method budget stated in evaluation calls, including confirmation calls.
5. Deployed artifact = last promoted candidate, not the best-looking dev score seen.

Operating characteristics (T=40, screen n=5, 10 iterations; sim_promotion.md): P(>=1 false promotion) 0.57 one-stage k=1 vs 0.04-0.08 under P; power +0.08: 0.85 vs 0.68-0.73; +0.10: 0.92 vs 0.87-0.90.

## 9. Cost accounting
All money metered from provider usage logs. Unpriced endpoint = "unknown", excluded from sums, sum flagged "lower bound", never treated as 0.
- Optimization cost C_opt(m,s): everything spent to produce champion (m,s): proposer + target rollouts on dev data (incl. screening and confirmation) + judges/simulators/diagnostics; tokens, USD, rollouts, wall hours; per seed with mean +- SD over seeds. Failed/aborted searches included.
- Cost per usable champion for "run S searches, deploy the best on dev" = S * mean C_opt; its accuracy is subject to best-of-S winner's curse, so it gets its own final-set row, not the per-seed mean.
- Deployment cost c_a: metered target cost of one deployed task attempt on the untouched final tasks under the same retry policy: mean over tasks of mean trial cost INCLUDING failures and retries; also cost per success c_a/p_a (ratio estimator, family-cluster bootstrap CI).
- Net value per task v(a) = R*p_a - c_a for a task reward R declared in advance (R=0: cost-only view, compare c_a at equal accuracy within the non-inferiority margin).
- Break-even volume V*(m,s) = C_opt(m,s) / (v(champion) - v(baseline)) tasks, defined only when the lower CI bound of the value difference is >0; otherwise "no break-even established" with the denominator's interval. Its CI combines the family-cluster bootstrap of the denominator with the empirical spread of the numerator over seeds; never a delta method when the denominator CI includes 0.
- Optimizer-model cost and deployed-target cost in separate columns; a combined column only when both are fully priced. Optimization cost and deployment savings are never netted into one number without both components shown.

## 10. Freeze list
Before any untouched-final scoring, hash and record: this plan; final-task manifest with family ids; model registry (provider, model id, snapshot/date, temperature, max tokens); evaluator and scorer code; the section 6 classifier; the section 5 comparison family; content hashes of all artifacts to be scored incl. the single baseline; analysis scripts at the commit used. Deviations after freeze go in decisions.md with reason and effect on interpretation; deviating analyses are labelled exploratory.
